# Terms S–Z

[Back to the term index](../termbase.md)

<a id="sachbedeutung"></a>
## Sachbedeutung

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** semantic change. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Sachbedeutung.

### 1. Sachbedeutung

**English:** substantive meaning / content meaning.

Original substantive meaning that can disappear so that a word functions only relationally.

**Rule:** Etymological origin alone does not settle current grammatical function.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:470 / PDF 480 / scan leaf 484](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=480); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> dass die ursprüngliche Sachbedeutung ganz untergegangen, kaum noch etymologisch zu erkennen ist

<a id="sachbegriff"></a>
## Sachbegriff

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** meaning, semantic content. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Sachbegriff, Sachbegriffe.

### 1. Object/content concept whose word can be repurposed for grammatical indication

**English:** object concept / content concept.

Object/content concept whose word can be repurposed for grammatical indication.

**Rule:** Distinguish the earlier lexical source from its grammatical use.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:421 / PDF 431 / scan leaf 435](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=431); main text; image-checked.

> grammatische, bloss die Verbindung der Rede bestimmende, nach Wörtern für Sachbegriffe.

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

### 2. Sachbegriff

**English:** substantive concept / content concept.

A content-bearing concept used to express a grammatical relation, as in the back/behind illustration.

**Rule:** Not necessarily a concrete physical object; keep conceptual content distinct from relation indicated as relation.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:463 / PDF 473 / scan leaf 477](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=473); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> ein grammatisches Verhältniss durch einen Sachbegriff ausgedruckt wird

**Reading note:** Image corrects the supplied OCR; both readings retained.

<a id="sachlich"></a>
## sachlich

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** gender. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** sachliche.

### 1. sachlich

**English:** neuter.

Neuter as the grammatical gender contrasted with feminine in the ship example.

**Rule:** Not factual, objective or matter-of-fact in this grammatical use.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:458 / PDF 468 / scan leaf 472](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=468); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> das Geschlecht, das weibliche oder sachliche

<a id="sachwort"></a>
## Sachwort

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** lexical and grammatical function. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Sachwörter.

### 1. Sachwort

**English:** content word / substantive word.

Originally content-bearing words that can become genuine relation-words.

**Rule:** Not restricted to nouns: the accompanying Chinese example is originally a verb.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:470 / PDF 480 / scan leaf 484](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=480); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> dass ursprüngliche Sachwörter in wirkliche Verhältnisswörter übergehen.

**English:** softness.

> seine Härte oder Sanftheit, seine Helligkeit oder Dumpfheit

<a id="satz"></a>
## Satz

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** discourse organization, discourse unit, units of speech. **Role:** contextual vocabulary, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Satz, Satzes, Sätze.

### 1. local sense

**English:** sentence.

A sentence whose elements can be traced through many categories and relations to display the interdependence of language.

**Rule:** Use sentence for the defined local use.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:394 / PDF 404 / scan leaf 408](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=404); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Jedes und Jedes durch Alles bestimmt wird, und dies ist buchstäblich wahr. Man kann an dem einfachsten Satz einen grossen Theil dieses Zusammenhanges sogar factisch nachweisen, wenn man den Satz nach allen Arten bei Sprachen möglicher Zergliederung in seine Elemente zerlegt, jedes Element in alle Kategorieen bringt, die

### 2. unit within discourse

**English:** sentence / clause.

Sentence or clause as a unit contributing weight to the meaning of discourse.

**Rule:** Choose the syntactic/discourse unit by context, not an isolated word.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:409 / PDF 419 / scan leaf 423](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=419); main text; image-checked.

> welches jedes Wort und jeder Satz in der Bedeutung der Rede haben,

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

### 3. proposition or assertion

**English:** proposition / claim.

The author’s preceding propositions/assertions about languages’ expressive capacity.

**Rule:** Not a count of finite clauses in this occurrence.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:433 / PDF 443 / scan leaf 447](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=443); main text; image-checked.

> Diese Sätze beruhen auf der Identität des Denkens und Sprechens.

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

### 4. Satz

**English:** sentence.

A simple or compound unit whose unity is supplied by relations of thought, distinguished from the Periode’s additional linguistic organization.

**Rule:** Default to sentence with an explicit local Satz/Periode explanation: thought-relations give Satz its unity, whereas Periode adds linguistic organization. Reserve proposition for an explicitly logical context.

**Caution:** Use proposition only where a logical-judgement use is explicitly at issue, such as the separately recorded assertion use;§136 itself supplies no modern truth-condition definition. Preserve the contrast with Periode, and do not infer a new sense from printed448’s unresolved same-Satz phrase.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:456 / PDF 466 / scan leaf 470](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=466); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> dass die Gedankenform zur Einheit des Satzes, des einfachen und zusammengesetzten, die Sprachform zur Einheit der Periode strebt.

**Comparative control:** [Adelung1811, Der Satz; §2(2)(a), logical use](https://lexika.digitale-sammlungen.de/adelung/lemma/bsb00009133_7_0_457). The logical sense is a verbally expressed judgement, with subject/predicate articulation; other senses include musical composition and physical setting. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

<a id="schaffen"></a>
## Schaffen

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** freedom, linguistic production. **Role:** contextual vocabulary, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Schaffen.

### 1. local sense

**English:** creating / creation.

Continual creation through freedom in humanity and individuals, preventing exhaustive prediction or derivation of language's course.

**Rule:** Use creating for the defined local use.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:398 / PDF 408 / scan leaf 412](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=408); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> selbstbestimmend werden. Darauf beruht es, dass in der Menschheit und in dem tieferregten Menschen ein unaufhörliches Schaffen lebendig ist, dass sich die Art ihres Seyns nie weder berechnen, noch erschöpfend erklären lässt, da jeder Richtung das unerklärbare Princip der Freiheit beigemischt bleibt. In den Sprachen rührt es

**Reading note:** Marginal section numeral excluded from continuous main-text quotation.

### 2. Creating sounds and concepts as distinguishable activities of linguistic growth, with different stated limits

**English:** creating / creation.

Creating sounds and concepts as distinguishable activities of linguistic growth, with different stated limits.

**Rule:** Do not merge sound-stock growth and conceptual development.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:434 / PDF 444 / scan leaf 448](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=444); main text; image-checked.

> Es ist ein Schaffen von Tönen und ein Schaffen von Begriffen,

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

<a id="scheinobject"></a>
## Scheinobject

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** epistemology. **Role:** conceptual term.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Scheinobjecte.

### 1. local sense

**English:** apparent object.

An apparent object or dream image from which a word must be distinguished by gaining reality in a hearer and respondent.

**Rule:** Use apparent object for the defined local use.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:381 / PDF 391 / scan leaf 395](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=391); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> ein Object, von ihm erzeugt und auf ihn zurückwirkend seyn. Damit es sich nun von einem blossen Scheinobjecte, einem Traumbilde, unterscheide, muss es Wesenheit in einem Hörenden und Erwiedernden gewinnen. Diesen Urtypus aller Sprachen drückt das Pronomen durch die Unterscheidung der zweiten Person von der dritten aus. Ich

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** exposition. **Role:** method term.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Schilderung.

### 1. local sense

**English:** description / sketch.

A sketch of language in operation requiring a certain unity, distinguished from separately conducted grammatical monographs.

**Rule:** Use description for the defined local use.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:372 / PDF 382 / scan leaf 386](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=382); main text; image-checked short quotation.

Denn die Schilderung einer Sprache in ihrem Wirken lässt sich nur in einer gewissen Einheit entwerfen, und selbst die Punkte, die auch hierbei einzeln ergründet werden müssen, stehen immer in näherer Beziehung zu

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** freedom. **Role:** contextual vocabulary.

### 1. local sense

**English:** limit / bound.

Limits of finitude within which freedom moves yet may become self-determining.

> so bleibt doch auch da dem Individuum ein gewisser Spielraum vorbehalten. Ueberall, wo die Freiheit sich in Schranken der Endlichkeit bewegt, giebt es zwar eine Reihe bestimmender, ihr im Augenblick des Wirkens fremder Einflüsse, sie kann aber auch, wie ein Blitz aus dem wolkenlosen Aether, plötzlich aus denselben

<a id="schrankenlosigkeit"></a>
## Schrankenlosigkeit

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** form and freedom. **Role:** conceptual term.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Schrankenlosigkeit.

### 1. local sense

**English:** unboundedness.

Thought's natural lack of limits, preserved through forms that enable passage into further forms.

**Rule:** Use unboundedness for the defined local use.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:370 / PDF 380 / scan leaf 384](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=380); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Aushauch des Lebendigen, immer nur so in feste Form zu beschränken, dass ihm dadurch selbst seine natürliche Schrankenlosigkeit, seine Freiheit in andre und andere überzugehen, gesichert wird. Denn die Begränzung so zu machen, dass die in ihr liegende Bestimmung durch sich selbst die bei ihr mögliche Beschränkung aufhebt,

<a id="schrei"></a>
## Schrei

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** sound. **Role:** contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Schrei.

### 1. local sense

**English:** cry.

The animal cry demanded by feeling, one of the motives united in articulated sound.

**Rule:** Use cry for the defined local use.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:376 / PDF 386 / scan leaf 390](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=386); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Es liegt aber hierin dreierlei: der Drang des Gedanken nach Aeusserung, das Bedürfniss der Empfindung zum thierischen Schrei, und die Nothwendigkeit der gesellschaftlichen Wechselwirkung zur Ausbildung des Gedanken. Jedes dieser Stücke führt einzeln zur Hervorbringung des Tons, und die Sprache vereinigt alle im articulirten Laut. Das Denken

<a id="schrift"></a>
## Schrift

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** writing. **Role:** contextual vocabulary, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Schrift.

### 1. local sense

**English:** writing.

Writing that gives intellectual activity an enduring body and can keep dormant thoughts reawakenable across generations.

**Rule:** Distinguish writing from spoken activity and from language's renewed existence in thought.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:374 / PDF 384 / scan leaf 388](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=384); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> wird durch den Ton in der Rede äusserlich und wahrnehmbar für die Sinne, und erhält durch die Schrift einen bleibenden Körper. Das auf diese Weise Erzeugte ist das Gesprochene und Aufgezeichnete aller Art, die Sprache aber der Inbegriff der Laute, welche die bestimmte intellectuelle Thätigkeit, welcher sie angehört,

### 2. Writing introduced as a condition of further systematization of an already spoken sound inventory

**English:** writing / script.

Writing introduced as a condition of further systematization of an already spoken sound inventory.

**Rule:** Distinguish the written system from the prior sound relations.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:403 / PDF 413 / scan leaf 417](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=413); main text; image-checked.

> Nach einmal entstandener Schrift und bei hinzutretender grammatischer Behandlung kann ein Alphabet

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

**Comparative control:** [printed 59–60; 69; PDF 72–73; 82; §8, Sprache; 9, The Work of the Mind](../../../sources/raw/references/underhill-2009-humboldt-worldview-language.pdf). Underhill emphasizes transient speaking acts, audible expression and the speaker’s hearing of the word. Writing preserves an incomplete representation that must be returned to living utterance. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

<a id="seele"></a>
## Seele

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** faculty vocabulary, faculty whole, human powers. **Role:** conceptual term, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Seele.

### 1. local sense

**English:** soul / mind.

The inward subject for which a thing becomes an object through a concept and the image produced by perception.

**Rule:** Preserve the local inward bearer without asserting a strict invariant faculty division from Geist.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:387 / PDF 397 / scan leaf 401](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=397); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> vollen Gehalt zu erschöpfen. Ebensowenig als ein Begriff ohne sie möglich ist, ebensowenig kann es für die Seele ein Gegenstand seyn, da ja jeder äussere Gegenstand nur vermittelst des Begriffes für sie Wesenheit erhält. In die Bildung und den Gebrauch der Sprache geht nothwendig die ganze Art der

### 2. The whole of mental powers involved in producing and understanding a word, including intellectual, sensory, and affective contributions

**English:** soul / mind.

The whole of mental powers involved in producing and understanding a word, including intellectual, sensory, and affective contributions.

**Rule:** Choose mind where soul would impose a religious doctrine; preserve the unified-powers account.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:420 / PDF 430 / scan leaf 434](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=430); main text; image-checked.

> die Gesammtkräfte der Seele zum Hervorbringen oder Verstehen desselben zusammenfasst.

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

### 3. Seele

**English:** soul / mind.

The inward human locus in which a grammatical view has its basis, posed as a problem of investigation.

**Rule:** Do not claim that this question itself establishes a distinct psychological faculty.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:455 / PDF 465 / scan leaf 469](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=465); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> was dasjenige in der Seele ist, worauf sie beruht

**Relations:** [Gesammtkraft](terms-f-k.md#gesammtkraft) (has_power: The account coordinates the soul’s combined powers in word production and understanding.).

**Comparative control:** [Adelung1811, 2. Die Seele; numbered homograph2, senses2–3](https://lexika.digitale-sammlungen.de/adelung/lemma/bsb00009134_2_0_117). Ranges from feeling/desiring capacity to the thinking being with understanding and will, and figuratively persons or animating essentials. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

### 2. Seyn

**English:** being.

<a id="sprache"></a>
## Sprache

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** language and activity, language theory, linguistic analysis. **Role:** conceptual term, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Sprache.

### 1. local sense

**English:** language.

Human language as the formative organ of thought and range of possible articulated sounds and combinations.

**Rule:** Preserve human-language, particular-language and activity/material standpoints rather than forcing one definition.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:374 / PDF 384 / scan leaf 388](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=384); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> dass die Sprache nicht bloss die Bezeichnung des, unabhängig von ihr geformten Gedanken, sondern selbst das bildende Organ des Gedanken ist.

### 2. inherited stock and system

**English:** language.

Historically accumulated stock of words and system of rules generated from speaking, becoming relatively independent power.

**Rule:** Retain the explicit shift from speaking to inherited language; do not deny the material standpoint.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:388 / PDF 398 / scan leaf 402](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=398); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Aus dem Sprechen aber erzeugt sich die Sprache, ein Vorrath von Wörtern und System von Regeln, und wächst, sich durch die Folge der Jahrtausende hinschlingend, zu einer von dem jedesmal Redenden, dem jedesmaligen Geschlecht, der Nation, ja zuletzt selbst von der Menschheit in gewisser Art unabhängigen Macht an.

### 3. individual language

**English:** language.

The particular language of each individual speaker, within shared national forms yet individually differentiated.

**Rule:** Keep this relative individualization compatible with the stated shared-forms sense.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:396 / PDF 406 / scan leaf 410](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=406); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Eine Nation hat freilich im Ganzen dieselbe Sprache, allein schon nicht alle ihre Mitglieder (38.) ganz dieselbe, und geht man noch weiter in das Feinste über, so besitzt jeder Mensch seine eigne.

### 4. Intellectual striving proceeding from thinking to thinking through minimally necessary articulated sound

**English:** language.

Intellectual striving proceeding from thinking to thinking through minimally necessary articulated sound.

**Rule:** Preserve the contrast with symbolic dwelling and the role of sound.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:429 / PDF 439 / scan leaf 443](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=439); main text; image-checked.

> Die Sprache dagegen ist ein rein geistiges Streben, gehend (24.) vom Denken zum Denken,

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

### 5. Sprache

**English:** language.

Language considered jointly as an inward human function and as already formed material confronting the individual.

**Rule:** Preserve both aspects; do not reduce language either to an inert object or to an unconstrained private act.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:462 / PDF 472 / scan leaf 476](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=472); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> die Sprache immer zugleich als eine innere menschliche Function und als einen sich ihm, als fremd, entgegenstellenden Stoff zu betrachten.

**Comparative control:** [printed 136; PDF 146](../../../sources/raw/references/humboldt-gs-08.pdf). Translations test, define and affect a language at a historical moment, and need repeated renewal rather than being permanent achievements. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Comparative control:** [printed 104–105; 110–112; PDF 3–4; 9–11; §6.1; 6.3.1](../../../sources/raw/references/manchester-1985/10.1075_sihols.32.c6.pdf). Manchester treats influence between language and national character as reciprocal, not a simple either/or. He separates inaccessible initial origins from observable interaction of already formed languages and communities, including inherited language character and new national influence. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Comparative control:** [printed 59–60; 69; PDF 72–73; 82; §8, Sprache; 9, The Work of the Mind](../../../sources/raw/references/underhill-2009-humboldt-worldview-language.pdf). Underhill emphasizes transient speaking acts, audible expression and the speaker’s hearing of the word. Writing preserves an incomplete representation that must be returned to living utterance. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Comparative control:** [Adelung1811, Die Sprache; abstract§1 and concrete§2](https://lexika.digitale-sammlungen.de/adelung/lemma/bsb00009134_2_1_1744). Separates speaking capacity and manner of expression from a shared language as an inventory/custom of words and expressions; broader and figurative uses are also listed. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

<a id="sprachform"></a>
## Sprachform

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** linguistic analysis, linguistic organization. **Role:** conceptual term, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Sprachform, Sprachformen.

### 1. form of a language and its expression

**English:** language-form / linguistic form.

Language’s form(s), considered as shared patterns admitting individual use and, in§136, as specifically linguistic organization distinct in analysis from thought-form.

**Rule:** In§50 preserve general forms and individualized use; in§136 preserve the extra linguistic conditions directed toward the Periode. Use the local bearer rather than counting plurality alone as a new lexical sense.

**Caution:** Do not insert inner where the source does not say it, or reduce Sprachform to morphology. The local thought/language distinction does not make two separable substances.

**Contextual uses retained within this sense:**

- Printed 396: Shared forms of a language whose generality allows different individual use. Use language form for the defined local use.
- Printed 456: Language’s own form, directing expression toward the Periode and adding specifically linguistic organization to thought-relations. Do not replace with inner form or reduce to morphology; keep distinct from Gedankenform.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:396 / PDF 406 / scan leaf 410](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=406); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> dem Gebiete auch der allgemeinsten Sprachuntersuchung verbannen. Denn es giebt mehrere Stufen, auf denen die Allgemeinheit der Sprachformen sich auf diese Weise individualisirt, und das individualisirende Princip ist dasselbe : das Denken und Sprechen in einer bestimmten Individualität. Dadurch entsteht die Verschiedenheit in der Sprache der Einzelnen, wie

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:456 / PDF 466 / scan leaf 470](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=466); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> die Sprachform zur Einheit der Periode strebt. In dem Satze ist das Princip der Einheit bloss das Gedankenverhältniss seiner Theile, in der Periode treten zu diesem

**Relations:** [Gedankenform](terms-f-k.md#gedankenform) (contrast: The Periode adds specifically linguistic conditions to thought-relations.).

**Comparative control:** [printed 49; PDF 59](../../../sources/raw/references/humboldt-gs-07-1.pdf). The account explicitly denies restricting linguistic form to grammatical form and treats the grammar/lexicon division as useful for learning rather than a boundary of research. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Comparative control:** [printed 83–86; PDF 18–21; §4.4, Form der Sprache](../../../sources/raw/references/manchester-1985/10.1075_sihols.32.c4.pdf). Manchester rejects both an exclusively generative-rule reading and an exclusively dynamic-force reading of Form. Something may form one item while itself being the formed product of another process; form/matter relations shift with the explanatory level. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** faculty vocabulary, human powers. **Role:** conceptual term, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

### 2. Sprachvermögen

**English:** capacity for language / linguistic capacity.

The inward capacity for language, parts of which can remain undeveloped if formal distinctions lack lively expression.

**Rule:** Do not identify the capacity with explicit grammatical knowledge or a modern biologically specified module.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:468 / PDF 478 / scan leaf 482](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=478); main text; image-checked short quotation.

**Comparative control:** [printed 80–83; PDF 15–18; §4.4](../../../sources/raw/references/manchester-1985/10.1075_sihols.32.c4.pdf). Manchester proposes a schema separating common linguistic capacities from nationally variable tendency, character and form. He treats Anlage as stronger than mere capacity and describes national character as a guiding disposition. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** linguistic analysis. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

### 1. stillschweigende Grammatik

**English:** tacit grammar / silent grammar.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:462 / PDF 472 / scan leaf 476](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=472); main text; image-checked short quotation.

**Relations:** [ausdrückliche Grammatik](terms-a-e.md#ausdrueckliche-grammatik) (contrast: The relation may be supplied by users or explicitly indicated in language.).

**Comparative control:** [printed 76–79; PDF 11–14; §4.3](../../../sources/raw/references/manchester-1985/10.1075_sihols.32.c4.pdf). Manchester says explicit and mentally supplied grammar occur within each language, not only as labels for opposed whole languages. He reconstructs universal forms through conceptual opposition and intermediacy, while questioning whether Humboldt means actual employment everywhere or potential availability. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

<a id="stoff"></a>
## Stoff

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** linguistic analysis, matter and form, process and product. **Role:** conceptual term, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Stoff, Stoffs.

### 1. local sense

**English:** material.

Inert material denied to exhaust language even as the available mass of words and rules.

**Rule:** Use material for what is available or acquired; do not let the inert-material denial erase the later inherited-material standpoint.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:369 / PDF 379 / scan leaf 381](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=379); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Eine Sprache kann nicht, wie ein Naturkörper, zerlegt werden, sie ist, auch nicht einmal in der durch sie gegebenen Masse von Wörtern und Regeln, ein daliegender Stoff, sondern eine Verrichtung, ein geistiger Process, wie das Leben ein körperlicher. Nichts, was sich auf sie bezieht, kann mit anatomischer, sondern nur mit physiologischer Behandlung verglichen werden, nichts in ihr ist statisch, alles dynamisch.

### 2. learning material

**English:** material.

The material acquired as a child understands more language, reinforcing the growing power of understanding.

**Rule:** Keep acquired material distinct from the capacity that increases with it.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:385 / PDF 395 / scan leaf 399](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=395); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> wachsendem Verhältniss, sondern in beständig sich selbst steigerndem, da die Erhöhung der Kraft und die Gewinnung des Stoffs sich gegenseitig verstärken und erweitern. Dass bei den Kindern nicht ein mechanisches Lernen der Sprache, sondern eine Entwicklung der Sprachkraft vorgeht, beweist auch, dass allen menschlichen Kräften ein gewisser Zeitpunkt

### 3. resistant material of sound formation

**English:** material / matter.

Resistant material that limits the spirit’s forming activity, here embodied sound.

**Rule:** State the local bearer rather than assuming all Stoff is acoustic.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:401 / PDF 411 / scan leaf 415](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=411); main text; image-checked.

> aber in diesem Uebergewicht immer wieder in etwas durch den widerstrebenden Stoff gehemmt wird.

**Reading note:** OCR quote preserves the exact searchable window. Checked quote is the explicitly bounded image-confirmed clause, with printed line divisions rejoined; margin numbers and apparatus outside that clause are excluded. Original OCR anomalies remain visible in ocr_quote.

### 4. words as material of discourse

**English:** material.

Words as the material out of which discourse is formed.

**Rule:** Distinct from sound as resistant material and from unnecessary conceptual matter.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:411 / PDF 421 / scan leaf 425](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=421); main text; image-checked.

> Alle, auch die zusammengesetzten Wörter sind die Bestandtheile, der Stoff der Rede,

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

### 5. sensory-affective material negated as material

**English:** material / matter.

Sensory and affective content regarded as material to be negated as such while retained formally.

**Rule:** Preserve the qualification as material; the text does not say all sensory contribution simply disappears.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:420 / PDF 430 / scan leaf 434](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=430); main text; image-checked.

> als Stoff vernichtet und als Form erhalten wird,

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

### 6. Stoff

**English:** material / matter.

Material content abstracted from grammatical speech-form, which adheres to content in a form-like way.

**Rule:** Keep the relative form/material distinction; do not assert a separate material substance.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:460 / PDF 470 / scan leaf 474](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=470); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> ganz entblösst von Stoff, an dem Inhalt nur wie eine Idee

**Relations:** [Form](terms-f-k.md#form) (related: Material and formal roles are locally specified rather than fixed sound/meaning buckets.).

**Comparative control:** [printed 49; PDF 59](../../../sources/raw/references/humboldt-gs-07-1.pdf). Nothing within language is absolutely unformed matter; an element can be matter relative to one operation while already formed in another respect. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Comparative control:** [printed 56; PDF 66](../../../sources/raw/references/humboldt-gs-07-1.pdf). Understanding and speaking are different effects of the same linguistic power. Understanding requires the hearer's own activity rather than receiving a transferred material content. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Comparative control:** [printed 83–86; PDF 18–21; §4.4, Form der Sprache](../../../sources/raw/references/manchester-1985/10.1075_sihols.32.c4.pdf). Manchester rejects both an exclusively generative-rule reading and an exclusively dynamic-force reading of Form. Something may form one item while itself being the formed product of another process; form/matter relations shift with the explanatory level. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Reading note:** Active main text freshly rechecked on printed429; not Creuzer quotation or deleted apparatus. Print line divisions rejoined.

### 2. symbolic embodiment in linguistic expression

**English:** symbol.

The symbol or sensible embodiment in which language casts thought, analytically distinguished from thought’s essence within a discussion that also insists on the synthetic unity of the two forms.

**Rule:** Retain symbol as the source word here, with its local thought/embodiment contrast. Do not treat the symbol as an arbitrary detachable label.

**Caution:** This is a contextual/metaphorical use, not proof that every Symbol occurrence has the strict natural-form definition of§101. In§136 the analytically distinguished forms are inseparable in actual language.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:455 / PDF 465 / scan leaf 469](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=465); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> nicht sowohl das Wesen des Gedanken, als das Symbol, in welches die Sprache den Gedanken fasst, angehende. Beide befinden sich im Gebiet der Grammatik, und lassen sich nicht von einander trennen, da die Sprache sie synthetisch in Eins verschmelzt,

**Reading note:** Image-confirmed text differs from supplied OCR; both retained.

**Relations:** [Wort](terms-s-z.md#wort) (contrast: Symbol has self-standing natural form; sound and concept are differently united in the word.).

### 3. audible sound purified for speech

<a id="treue"></a>
## Treue

**Scope:** supporting translation-policy source. **Domains:** translation policy. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Treue.

### 1. faithfulness to the original’s character

**English:** faithfulness / fidelity.

The translation principle directed to the original’s true character rather than its accidental details at the cost of that character.

**Rule:** Use faithfulness/fidelity in this translation-policy comparison; preserve its specified object, the original’s character.

**Caution:** This is Humboldt’s Agamemnon policy, not a verified technical headword of Grundzüge or a command to copy German syntax. Do not identify it without argument with Nord’s later ethical loyalty.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS VIII:132 / PDF 142](../../../sources/raw/references/humboldt-gs-08.pdf#page=142); main text; coordinator image-checked short quotation.

> Diese Treue muss auf den wahren Charakter des Originals, nicht, mit Verlassung jenes, auf seine Zufälligkeiten gerichtet seyn,

**Reading note:** Supporting translation-policy source, outside Grundzüge. Print line-end divisions rejoined; footnote callouts omitted. No exact locator in the main-text OCR stream is claimed.

**Relations:** [das Fremde](terms-a-e.md#das-fremde) (related: The ensuing discussion connects fidelity to the foreign element perceived in translation.).

**Comparative control:** [printed 130; PDF 140](../../../sources/raw/references/humboldt-gs-08.pdf). A translation striving harder for faithfulness can become more divergent, since its counterpart for each fine particularity remains different. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Comparative control:** [printed 132; PDF 142](../../../sources/raw/references/humboldt-gs-08.pdf). Faithfulness concerns the original's true character rather than incidental features at the cost of that character. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Comparative control:** [printed 131–135, 139–143; PDF 1–5, 9–13](../../../sources/raw/references/nord-2006-purposeful-activity.pdf). A translation brief identifies purpose and reader; documentary/instrumental orientation depends on that brief. Contextual explanation can be separated from translation, and loyalty constrains a merely purpose-based solution. Nord’s purpose/loyalty framework is a later translation-policy control, not a lexical equivalent or historical definition of Humboldt’s Treue.

### 2. Uebung

<a id="ursprache"></a>
## Ursprache

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** historical origins. **Role:** conceptual term.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Ursprache.

### 1. local sense

**English:** original language / primordial language.

An original language whose single or multiple existence Humboldt considers possible but not established or needed for explaining observed language phenomena.

**Rule:** Do not equate general human language or contact-connected unity with one original language.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:392 / PDF 402 / scan leaf 406](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=402); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Dunkel verliert, so tritt bei dieser Unkenntniss ihrer Zeitfolge für uns gewissermassen Gleichzeitigkeit derselben ein. Eine Ursprache anzunehmen, oder eine Verschiedenheit der Sprachen vor und nach der Vertilgung des grössten Theils des Menschengeschlechts durch eine Flut aufzustellen, scheint mir unnütz und misleitend. Weder das Gebiet der Thatsachen,

**Reading note:** Image correction: 'gewässermassen' → 'gewissermassen'. Marginal section numeral excluded from continuous main-text quotation.

**Relations:** [Urtypus](terms-s-z.md#urtypus) (distinguished_from: Original language differs from the fundamental I/Thou pattern.).

<a id="ursprung"></a>

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** historical method, historical origins. **Role:** contextual vocabulary, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Ursprung.

### 1. local sense

**English:** origin.

The origin of American peoples and languages, left unspecified while their later internal history motivates continental study.

**Rule:** Use origin for the defined local use.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:364 / PDF 374 / scan leaf 376](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=374); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Welches daher auch der Ursprung

### 2. Historical origin of the language or its forms, whose remoteness limits direct investigation

**English:** origin.

Historical origin of the language or its forms, whose remoteness limits direct investigation.

**Rule:** Do not turn inaccessible beginnings into established reconstruction.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:414 / PDF 424 / scan leaf 428](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=424); main text; image-checked.

> und der Ursprung sich in Dunkel verliert,

Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

<a id="urspruenglich"></a>
## ursprünglich

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** genetic explanation, historical qualification. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** ursprünglich, ursprüngliche, ursprünglichere.

### 1. Relatively earlier or less transformed, expressly glossed here as retaining fewer traces of major upheaval

**English:** more original / less transformed.

Relatively earlier or less transformed, expressly glossed here as retaining fewer traces of major upheaval.

**Rule:** Do not make this mean directly attested primordial language.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:417 / PDF 427 / scan leaf 431](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=427); main text; image-checked.

> ursprünglichere, d. h. weniger Spuren grosser Umwälzungen an sich tragende Sprachen

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

### 2. ursprünglich

**English:** originally / primordially.

Originally or primordially given in the proposed natural order, rather than necessarily earliest historically attested.

**Rule:** Use originally for the attested ursprünglich here.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:447 / PDF 457 / scan leaf 461](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=457); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Die Rede, und nicht das Wort ist das in der Natur zuerst und ursprünglich Gedachte.

### 3. historically earlier meaning

**English:** original / earlier.

Original or earlier in the semantic history of a word, potentially lost in its present relational use.

**Rule:** Do not make the original content necessarily active in present meaning or confuse historical earlier meaning with the primordial order of §129.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:470 / PDF 480 / scan leaf 484](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=480); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> die ursprüngliche Sachbedeutung ganz untergegangen

**Comparative control:** [printed 75–79; PDF 88–92; §10, Form](../../../sources/raw/references/underhill-2009-humboldt-worldview-language.pdf). Underhill stresses that known languages reshape inherited material; form is maintained through recurring activity and is partly constant and partly fluid. Details and whole organization must be studied together, with their historical specificity retained. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

<a id="urtheilskraft"></a>
## Urtheilskraft

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** faculty vocabulary. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Urtheilskraft.

### 1. Urtheilskraft

**English:** faculty of judgment.

The faculty of judgment supporting the pure-thinking aspect of language, alongside understanding.

**Rule:** Keep distinct from Verstand and from creative imagination.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:455 / PDF 465 / scan leaf 469](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=465); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Was in der Sprache reines Denken ist, beruht auf der Urtheilskraft und dem Verstände

**Relations:** [Verstand](terms-s-z.md#verstand) (related: Both support pure thinking; separate faculty nouns are retained.).

<a id="urtypus"></a>
## Urtypus

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** communication, linguistic analysis. **Role:** conceptual term, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Urtypus.

### 1. local sense

**English:** fundamental pattern.

The fundamental I/Thou pattern of speaking expressed in second- versus third-person pronouns.

**Rule:** Do not render as proto-language or primordial language.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:381 / PDF 391 / scan leaf 395](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=391); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> von einem blossen Scheinobjecte, einem Traumbilde, unterscheide, muss es Wesenheit in einem Hörenden und Erwiedernden gewinnen. Diesen Urtypus aller Sprachen drückt das Pronomen durch die Unterscheidung der zweiten Person von der dritten aus. Ich und Er sind wirklich verschiedne Gegenstände, und mit ihnen ist eigentlich Alles erschöpft,

**Reading note:** Image correction: 'E r' → 'Er'.

### 2. Urtypus

**English:** original pattern / fundamental pattern.

The original pattern of a language’s period construction resting in the mind and becoming conscious through use.

**Rule:** Not a reconstructed proto-language or a natural class of languages; preserve the mental pattern and its realization.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:467 / PDF 477 / scan leaf 481](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=477); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> dessen Form, als Urtypus, in dem Geiste ruht

**Reading note:** Image corrects the supplied OCR; both readings retained.

**Comparative control:** [printed 312; PDF 5; §3, Language](../../../sources/raw/references/trabant-2023-anthropological-linguistics.pdf). Trabant presents language as forming cognition rather than merely conveying independently ready concepts, while making the other person and reciprocal speaker/hearer exchange integral to that activity. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Verbalform.

**English:** verbal form.

A verbal form as a grammatical realization, which itself would require existential designation if every relation demanded a separate sign.

**Rule:** Keep realized form distinct from the category Verbum and from the principle of positing.

<a id="verbalnatur"></a>

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** word class and realization. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Verbalnatur.

### 1. Verbalnatur

**English:** verbal nature / verbal status.

A word’s verbal status or nature as established either by grammatical designation or by customary use.

**Rule:** Do not equate conventional verb use with an overt morphological verb marker.

> ob die Verbalnatur in einer grammatischen Bezeichnung, oder in der Gewohnheit des Sprachgebrauchs liegt.

<a id="verbalsinn"></a>

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Verbalsinn.

The original verbal meaning of a word, potentially forgotten as it serves a relational function.

**Reading note:** Image correction: 'Üectirte' → 'flectirte'.

**English:** verb.

The verb considered as naming an action, distinguished from its specifically grammatical connecting function.

### 4. verb as grammatical connector

**English:** verb.

The verb as the connecting means of the Satz, the specifically grammatical view.

**Rule:** Preserve the distinction from action meaning; the author’s criterion is grammatical function.

A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

<a id="verbum-seyn"></a>
## Verbum seyn

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** verbal realization. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

### 1. Verbum seyn

**English:** verb be / verb to be.

<a id="vereinigung"></a>

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** communication. **Role:** contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Vereinigung.

### 1. local sense

**English:** association / union.

Social association as the strongest means of approaching truth and measuring one's distance from it.

<a id="verfahren"></a>
## Verfahren

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** linguistic operations, method, method and activity. **Role:** contextual vocabulary, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Verfahren, Verfahrens.

### 1. local sense

**English:** procedure / way of operating.

The overall way a language uses and treats its means, distinguished non-rigidly from its constituents.

**Rule:** Use procedure or way of operating for how language coordinates and applies its means; preserve the expressly non-rigid opposition to constituents.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:370 / PDF 380 / scan leaf 384](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=380); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Mittel, nach der Gesammtbehandlung, die sie erfahren, und ergründet in möglicher Vollständigkeit von allen Seiten aus das Verfahren der Sprache im Ganzen, von dessen Darstellung erst die wahre Durchschauung ihres Wesens abhängt. Von den Punkten, welche hierbei in Betrachtung kommen, lassen sich zwar leicht mehrere beispielsweise angeben. So

### 2. expository procedure

**English:** procedure.

A choice of publication/expository procedure: present general conclusions before separate grammars and dictionaries.

**Rule:** Do not turn an author's publication procedure into the language's internal activity.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:366 / PDF 376 / scan leaf 378](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=376); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Darstellung der aus den Forschungen über die einzelnen Sprachen gezogenen allgemeinen Folgerungen vorangehen zu lassen. Dies letztere Verfahren hat den Nachtheil, dass der Leser, dem die Hülfsmittel zu dem Studium der einzelnen Sprachen, bei der Seltenheit und der oft sehr unbequemen Einrichtung der vorhandenen, erst geschaffen werden müssen,

### 3. Procedure of inquiry, here comparing designating and designated concepts in both directions

**English:** procedure.

Procedure of inquiry, here comparing designating and designated concepts in both directions.

**Rule:** Distinguish the analyst’s procedure from the language’s own way of forming expressions.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:440 / PDF 450 / scan leaf 454](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=450); main text; image-checked.

> Die Vergleichung dieses doppelten Verfahrens zeigt alsdann theils die Angemessenheit der Bezeichnung,

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

### 4. analytical procedure

**English:** procedure / manner of proceeding.

A manner of proceeding in analysis which can misrepresent a language if it conflates supplied and intrinsic grammar.

**Rule:** Distinguish the analyst’s method from a language’s own operation when the bearer changes.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:471 / PDF 481 / scan leaf 485](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=481); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> bei einem solchen Verfahren theils verkannt, und sogar verdreht

### 5. language’s grammatical procedure

**English:** procedure / manner of operating.

A language’s overall grammatical way of proceeding, contrasted with treatment of individual grammatical parts.

**Rule:** Not merely the analyst’s list of forms or a fixed typological class.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:462 / PDF 472 / scan leaf 476](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=472); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> auf das ganze grammatische Verfahren überhaupt beziehen könne.

**Relations:** [Bestandtheil](terms-a-e.md#bestandtheil) (distinguished_from: Overall use of means is non-rigidly opposed to constituents.).

**Comparative control:** [printed 314–316; PDF 7–9; §4.1.2–4.1.3](../../../sources/raw/references/trabant-2023-anthropological-linguistics.pdf). Trabant distinguishes recurring morphosyntactic procedures from rigid natural classes of languages. He also explicitly recognizes Humboldt’s obsolete ranking of inflection by its philosophical fit to synthesis, and situates that ranking alongside the value and fundamental equality of all languages. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**English:** manner of proceeding / procedure.

A general manner of proceeding in grammatical designation, subject to many further determinations.

A mode of proceeding that defines form operationally.

<a id="vergessenheit"></a>
## Vergessenheit

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** semantic change. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Vergessenheit.

### 1. Vergessenheit

**English:** oblivion / being forgotten.

The forgetting of earlier substantive content in a grammatical word’s current relational use.

**Rule:** Not an assertion of a conscious decision to erase meaning; the transition is historical and gradual.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:464 / PDF 474 / scan leaf 478](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=474); main text; image-checked short quotation.

<a id="vergleichendes-sprachstudium"></a>
## vergleichendes Sprachstudium

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** method. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** vergleichenden Sprachstudium.

### 1. vergleichendes Sprachstudium

**English:** comparative study of language.

Comparative study of language whose grammatical basis depends on distinguishing grammar in language from grammar supplied by users.

**Rule:** Not only genealogical comparison or a fixed typological classification.

> allem vergleichenden Sprachstudium, insofern es sich auf Grammatik bezieht

<a id="verhaeltnissbezeichnung"></a>
## Verhältnissbezeichnung

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** designation. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Verhältnissbezeichnung.

### 1. Verhältnissbezeichnung

**English:** relation designation / designation of a relation.

Designation of a relation as relation, visibly distinct in the argument from designating a substantive concept.

**Rule:** Do not equate the relation, its designation and the sound that carries the designation.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:469 / PDF 479 / scan leaf 483](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=479); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> den Unterschied zwischen Begriffs- und Verhältnissbezeichnung offenbar macht.

**Comparative control:** [Manchester1985 chapter7, printed137–138,141; PDF14–15,18](../../../sources/raw/references/manchester-1985/10.1075_sihols.32.c7.pdf). Manchester reconstructs a tendency to reserve meaning for conceptual content while acknowledging relational grammatical significance; pure formality marks a relation without additional substantive content. He distinguishes this reconstruction from communicative adequacy and ideological superiority. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

<a id="verhaeltnisswort"></a>

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** grammatical realization, lexical classification. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

Relational word named in a suspended compound with Beziehungswörter.

### 2. Verhältnisswort

**English:** relation-word / relational word.

A relation-word needed to connect a substantive concept with its principal word, even when the concept itself stands for a relation.

**Rule:** Distinguish the relational word from the Sachbegriff it relates; do not equate mental requirement with an audible word.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:463 / PDF 473 / scan leaf 477](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=473); main text; image-checked short quotation.

<a id="verkehr"></a>

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** historical connection. **Role:** contextual vocabulary.

Actual interaction of speakers through which similarities can be transmitted from already formed language.

A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

Language as an operation or performance rather than a ready-made material stock.

**Rule:** Use operation for the defined local use.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:369 / PDF 379 / scan leaf 381](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=379); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Eine Sprache kann nicht, wie ein Naturkörper, zerlegt werden, sie ist, auch nicht einmal in der durch sie gegebenen Masse von Wörtern und Regeln, ein daliegender Stoff, sondern eine Verrichtung, ein geistiger Process, wie das Leben ein körperlicher. Nichts, was sich auf sie bezieht, kann mit anatomischer, sondern nur mit physiologischer Behandlung verglichen werden, nichts in ihr ist statisch, alles dynamisch.

### 2. Verrichtung

**English:** operation / activity.

An operation or performance; speaking is expressly denied to be strictly a merely organic bodily operation.

**Rule:** Retain the denial that speaking is an organic operation in the strict bodily sense.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:451 / PDF 461 / scan leaf 465](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=461); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> das Reden kann niemals im eigentlichen Verstände eine organische Verrichtung genannt werden.

Wo man nun einzelne Sprachen zu schildern versucht, muss man von dem allgemeinen Typus dieses Processes ausgehen und dahin zurückkehren, weil es

<a id="verschiedenheit"></a>
## Verschiedenheit

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** individuality, linguistic analysis. **Role:** contextual vocabulary, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Verschiedenheit.

### 1. local sense

**English:** difference / diversity.

The small but pervasive difference between individual understandings of a word, recurring through each person's whole language.

**Rule:** Use difference for the defined local use.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:396 / PDF 406 / scan leaf 410](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=406); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> seine eigne. Bei jedem Denken und Empfinden kehrt, vermöge der Einerleiheit der Individualitaet, dieselbe Verschiedenheit zurück,

### 2. Verschiedenheit

**English:** diversity / difference.

Diversity or difference of grammatical views and procedures among languages, beyond merely different sounds.

**Rule:** Identify what differs; do not reduce all diversity to sound, fixed classes or value ranking.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:445 / PDF 455 / scan leaf 459](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=455); main text; image-checked short quotation.

<a id="verstand"></a>
## Verstand

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** faculty, faculty vocabulary, human powers. **Role:** conceptual term, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Verstand, Verstände.

### 1. faculty of understanding

**English:** understanding / intellect.

Understanding as an intellectual faculty engaged in analysis, distinctions and thought, locally contrasted with other faculties such as creative imagination or synthesizing reason.

**Rule:** Prefer understanding where faculty contrasts matter. In im Verstande des Redenden the possessor identifies the speaker’s faculty; it is not the idiom in the sense.

**Caution:** Different activities of this faculty are retained as contextual uses, not counted as new lexical senses. The separate sense-of-expression idiom requires its own construction and context.

**Contextual uses retained within this sense:**

- Printed 395: Analysing understanding/intellect unable exhaustively to represent what becomes felt in language use. Use understanding/intellect for the analytical faculty. Use sense only when the construction concerns meaning or an interpretation, as in im strengsten Verstände; not every prepositional im Verstande phrase is idiomatic.
- Printed 410, 432: Understanding as the faculty performing distinctions and logical constructions, including separating linguistic elements and constructing technical concepts. This occurrence does not mean the sense of a word or expression.
- Printed 455, 462: Understanding as a faculty supporting pure thought, distinct from creative imagination’s embodiment in language. Use understanding for this faculty. The possessed construction im Verstande des Redenden also names the speaker’s understanding, not the sense of an expression.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:395 / PDF 405 / scan leaf 409](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=405); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Es tritt aber bei dem eignen Gebrauch auch Alles das in das Gefühl und somit in die Erkenntniss, was durch den zergliedernden Verstand nie darstellbar ist, und so entspringt aus dem durch erweiterten Ueberblick geleiteten Gebrauch auch erweiterte Einsicht.

**Reading note:** Image correction: 'U eberblick' → 'Ueberblick'.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:410 / PDF 420 / scan leaf 424](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=420); main text; image-checked.

> indem der Verstand die Grundtheile der Sprache genauer trennt und bestimmter festhält,

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:432 / PDF 442 / scan leaf 446](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=442); main text; image-checked.

> die nur logischen, durch den Verstand construirten,

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR:

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:455 / PDF 465 / scan leaf 469](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=465); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Was in der Sprache reines Denken ist, beruht auf der Urtheilskraft und dem Verstände

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:462 / PDF 472 / scan leaf 476](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=472); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> als Typus im Verstände des Redenden ruhende

### 2. sense or interpretation in a qualifying idiom

**English:** sense.

The meaning or interpretation in which something is taken, as in the strictest or proper sense, rather than the intellectual faculty of a person.

**Rule:** Use sense for the cited qualifying idioms im strengsten/eigentlichen Verstände. A possessed phrase such as im Verstande des Redenden refers instead to the speaker’s understanding.

**Caution:** The preposition im alone is not a diagnostic. Syntax, possessor and the surrounding argument decide the local use.

**Contextual uses retained within this sense:**

- Printed 392: Sense in the idiom im strengsten Verstände, qualifying one possible concept of an original language. Use sense here rather than the faculty understanding.
- Printed 451: Sense or meaning in which an expression is used, here restricting the label organic operation. Render the idiomatic im eigentlichen Verstände as in the proper/strict sense; do not treat this occurrence as the faculty of understanding.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:392 / PDF 402 / scan leaf 406](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=402); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Begriffe vermag dahin zu führen. Es mag Eine, es mag mehrere Ursprachen gegeben haben , im strengsten Verstände, wenn das Menschengeschlecht mehr als Einen geographischen Anfangspunkt hat, in einem weniger strengen, wenn, und dies könnten gerade grosse Fluthen hervorbringen, Völker so vereinzelt worden wären, dass sich alle Spur

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:451 / PDF 461 / scan leaf 465](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=461); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> im eigentlichen Verstände eine organische Verrichtung genannt werden.

**Relations:** [Vernunft](terms-s-z.md#vernunft) (contrast: Technical logical construction versus synthesizing philosophical reason on432.).

**Comparative control:** [Adelung1811, Der Verstand; subjective faculty and objective meaning/sense](https://lexika.digitale-sammlungen.de/adelung/lemma/bsb00009134_5_0_911). The entry separates understanding as capacity (including broad and narrower faculties) from objective sense/meaning of words or discourse. It distinguishes the narrower faculty from Vernunft but reports variable ordinary usage. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

<a id="verstehen"></a>

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** communication, understanding. **Role:** contextual vocabulary, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

### 2. qualified interpersonal understanding

### 2. human affinity

<a id="vorstellung"></a>
## Vorstellung

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** cognition, faculty and content. **Role:** conceptual term, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Vorstellung.

### 1. local sense

**English:** representation.

Representation that cannot become a concept without thinking's connection with sound.

**Rule:** Use representation where it can become a concept through the sound-linked process; distinguish representation, concept, object impression and word effect.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:375 / PDF 385 / scan leaf 389](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=385); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Nothwendigkeit geknüpft, eine Verbindung mit dem Ton einzugehen, das Denken kann sonst nicht zur Deutlichkeit gelangen, die Vorstellung nicht zum Begriff werden. **Reading note:** Image correction: 'Begrift" A\'f?' → 'Begriff'.

### 2. subject–object formation

**English:** representation.

A representation formed through sensory activity and inner action, separating as an object and being taken back into the subject.

**Rule:** Preserve the synthetic sensory/inner formation rather than a mere copy of an already present object.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:377 / PDF 387 / scan leaf 391](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=387); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Die Thätigkeit der Sinne muss sich mit der inneren Handlung des Geistes synthetisch verbinden, und aus dieser Verbindung reisst sich die Vorstellung los, wird, der subjectiven Kraft gegenüber, zum Object, und kehrt, als solches aufs neue wahrgenommen, in jene zurück.

### 3. The individual representation of an object occasioned by a word, distinguished from the object itself

**English:** representation / conception.

The individual representation of an object occasioned by a word, distinguished from the object itself.

**Rule:** Do not silently translate as concept or physical image in all cases.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:418 / PDF 428 / scan leaf 432](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=428); main text; image-checked.

> Diese Vorstellung muss von dem Gegenstande unterschieden werden;

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

### 4. Vorstellung

**English:** representation / conception.

A mental representation of the possible infinite variety of grammatical arrangements, gained through broad use of a language.

**Rule:** Not necessarily a sensory picture or a fixed technical stage in a Kantian taxonomy.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:459 / PDF 469 / scan leaf 473](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=469); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> so bildet sich eine Vorstellung der möglichen unendlichen Mannigfaltigkeit.

**Relations:** [Gegenstand](terms-f-k.md#gegenstand) (contrast: Individual representation is abstractly distinguished from its object.); [Begriff](terms-a-e.md#begriff) (related: Representation and completed concept must not be silently made synonyms.).

**Comparative control:** [printed 53; PDF 63](../../../sources/raw/references/humboldt-gs-07-1.pdf). Language is presented as formative for thought; connection with articulated sound makes inner activity perceptible and is required for representation to become concept. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Comparative control:** [printed 55; PDF 65](../../../sources/raw/references/humboldt-gs-07-1.pdf). The produced representation becomes an object returning to its own subject; this does not remove subjectivity. Language is necessary to this return and to concept formation. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Comparative control:** [Adelung1811, Die Vorstellung; §2(3), mental use](https://lexika.digitale-sammlungen.de/adelung/lemma/bsb00009134_5_1_1841). Can be a mental image, and more broadly a concept or idea; the entry distinguishes the act and what is represented. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Comparative control:** [printed 72–73; PDF 85–86; §9, The Work of the Mind](../../../sources/raw/references/underhill-2009-humboldt-worldview-language.pdf). Underhill rejects an inference from linguistically mediated conceptualization to the nonexistence of an external world. He differentiates real things from objects as organized for understanding and warns against misleading aphoristic formulations. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

<a id="weltansicht"></a>
## Weltansicht

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** language and thought, perspective. **Role:** conceptual term, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Weltansicht.

### 1. view or standpoint of the world

**English:** view of the world / world-view.

A view/standpoint of the world belonging to an individual and further shaped and intensified through language; a language’s connected presentation can thus have its own distinctive world-view.

**Rule:** Preserve the individual standpoint already possible independently of language in§39 and the additional contribution made by language. Keep individual and language bearers explicit.

**Caution:** The uses do not imply identical beliefs or one ideology for all speakers. The later Weltansicht/Weltanschauung interpretive distinction is a control, not an invariant lexical law proved here.

**Contextual uses retained within this sense:**

- Printed 387: A view of the world: each individual already has a standpoint through subjective perception independently of language; language intensifies this and contributes its connected word-sound effects, yielding a language-specific world-view. Preserve both the individual standpoint already independent of language and its intensification through language; do not make all perspective originate in language.
- Printed 420: The particular view of the world afforded through a language’s ways of presenting objects. Not a claim here that all speakers have identical beliefs.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:387 / PDF 397 / scan leaf 401](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=397); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Da aller objectiven Wahrnehmung unvermeidlich Subjectivitaet beigemischt ist, so kann man schon unabhängig von der Sprache jede menschliche Individualität als einen eignen Standpunkt der Weltansicht betrachten. Sie wird aber noch viel mehr dazu durch die Sprache, da das Wort sich, der Seele gegenüber, auch wieder selbst zum Object macht, und eine neue, vom Subject sich absondernde Eigenthümlichkeit hinzubringt,

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:420 / PDF 430 / scan leaf 434](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=430); main text; image-checked.

> dass jede Sprache eine eigne Weltansicht gewährt.

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

**Relations:** [Weltvorstellung](terms-s-z.md#weltvorstellung) (related: Different observed formulations kept searchable; no invariant semantic opposition is inferred merely from different nouns.).

**Comparative control:** [printed 55–57; PDF 68–70; §7, Worldview](../../../sources/raw/references/underhill-2009-humboldt-worldview-language.pdf). Following Trabant, Underhill distinguishes language-bound conceptual world-presentation from beliefs or ideologies which may conflict inside one language and migrate between languages. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Comparative control:** [printed 72–73; PDF 85–86; §9, The Work of the Mind](../../../sources/raw/references/underhill-2009-humboldt-worldview-language.pdf). Underhill rejects an inference from linguistically mediated conceptualization to the nonexistence of an external world. He differentiates real things from objects as organized for understanding and warns against misleading aphoristic formulations. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Comparative control:** [printed 322–323; PDF 15–16; §5.2.2](../../../sources/raw/references/trabant-2023-anthropological-linguistics.pdf). Trabant contrasts Humboldt’s open and limited influence of linguistic perspective with deterministic reception, especially Weisgerber’s closed collective image. He invokes shared human dispositions, common reality and speakers’ capacity to resist and change languages. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

<a id="wendung"></a>
## Wendung

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** linguistic analysis. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Wendungen.

### 1. Wendung

**English:** turn / formulation.

A turn or formulation of expression through which the thought itself is modified.

**English:** being / entity.

A being/entity made by the inseparable unity of word-sound and concept.

**English:** nature / constitution.

The full nature/constitution of an object, which a word cannot supply exhaustively.

> in dem ganzen Wesen desselben gegeben werden;

The whole human being in its capacity to be affected by language.
