<a id="wort"></a>
## Wort

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** lexicon, linguistic analysis, word and thought. **Role:** contextual vocabulary, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Wort, Worts.

### 1. local sense

**English:** word.

A word born from subjective perception whose sound adds a third contribution to a concept beyond object impression and subjective reception.

**Rule:** Distinguish the word, the sound carrying it, its concept and the object; keep plural choices responsive to context.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:387 / PDF 397 / scan leaf 401](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=397); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> da das Wort sich, der Seele gegenüber, auch wieder selbst zum Object macht, und eine neue, vom Subject sich absondernde Eigenthümlichkeit hinzubringt, so dass nunmehr in dem Begriffe ein Dreifaches liegt, der Eindruck des Gegenstandes, die Art der Aufnahme desselben im Subject, die Wirkung des Worts, als Sprachlaut.

### 2. generative articulation

**English:** word.

An articulated word presented by its form as a part of an indefinitely generative whole language.

**Rule:** Retain the word's internal elements and relations to further words rather than treating it only as an indivisible signal.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:384 / PDF 394 / scan leaf 398](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=394); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Nun aber ist dasjenige, was die Articulation dem blossen Hervorrufen seiner Bedeutung (welches natürlich auch durch sie in höherer Vollkommenheit geschieht) hinzufügt, dass sie das Wort unmittelbar durch seine Form als einen Theil eines unendlichen Ganzen, einer Sprache, darstellt.

### 3. sound-concept linguistic individual

**English:** word.

A linguistic individual uniting sound and concept; the simple word contains the complete intellectual action attributed to language.

**Rule:** Retain Worte/Wörter as observed forms without importing a dictionary-only distribution.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:410 / PDF 420 / scan leaf 424](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=420); main text; image-checked.

> Ein Wort ist ein Laut, der einen Begriff bezeichnet.

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

### 4. word actualized in thought or speech

**English:** word.

The actual word is a current thought or spoken realization, contrasted with its inherited shell.

**Rule:** Preserve the distinction from a dead inherited form.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:422 / PDF 432 / scan leaf 436](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=432); main text; image-checked.

> Denn nur das wirklich gedachte oder gesprochene ist das eigentliche Wort,

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

### 5. Wort

**English:** word.

A word usable as an incomplete expression whose full sense must be supplied; words are also parts separated from connected speech.

**Rule:** Use word as a separable component or an utterance capable of contextual completion.

**Caution:** Do not reverse §129’s proposed whole-to-parts order or make dictionary isolation the word’s entire existence.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:446 / PDF 456 / scan leaf 460](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=456); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Selbst wenn er ein einzelnes Wort ausspricht, thut er es im nur zu ergänzenden Sinn einer Redensart

**Relations:** [Laut](terms-l-r.md#laut) (constitutive_aspect: Sound is the word’s sensible contribution in§72; the word is treated as a unity, not as an independently complete sound plus an independently finished concept.); [Begriff](terms-a-e.md#begriff) (related: The word designates and helps complete the concept.); [Element](terms-a-e.md#element) (contrast: A word contains the full act; analyzed syllable/letter are elements.).

**Comparative control:** [printed 306; PDF 316](../../../sources/raw/references/humboldt-gs-04.pdf). Unity and the dominance of the word over added subsidiary sounds define form in this passage. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Comparative control:** [printed 129; PDF 139](../../../sources/raw/references/humboldt-gs-08.pdf). The word is constitutive of the formation and retention of a concept, not merely its subsequent sign. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Comparative control:** [printed 323; PDF 333](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf). The author combines the impossibility of thinking without language with a person's distinction of thought from word and movement beyond the bounds of currently given language. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

**Comparative control:** [Adelung1811, Das Wort; §1 and§2](https://lexika.digitale-sammlungen.de/adelung/lemma/bsb00009134_6_2_2820). The entry distinguishes isolated lexical items, plural Wörter, from words in connected speech, plural Worte; it discusses borderline examples explicitly. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

<a id="wortbildung"></a>
## Wortbildung

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** linguistic operations, word formation. **Role:** contextual vocabulary, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Wortbildung.

### 1. local sense

**English:** word formation.

Word formation which has rules of its own, blocking an equation of words/matter with sounds alone.

**Rule:** Use word formation for the defined local use.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:399 / PDF 409 / scan leaf 413](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=409); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> der Sprache. Man muss diese Eintheilung nicht verwechseln mit der in Laute und Regeln; denn auch die Wortbildung kennt Regeln, und die Redeverbindung bedient sich bisweilen ihr eigenthümlicher Laute ; man muss vielmehr, die Rede als den Zweck der Sprache betrachtend, diese Classification dergestalt in ihrer wahren Natur

### 2. Formation of words under a language’s rules and habits, not necessarily recovery of absolute origins

**English:** word formation.

Formation of words under a language’s rules and habits, not necessarily recovery of absolute origins.

**Rule:** Distinguish from combining existing words in discourse.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:413 / PDF 423 / scan leaf 427](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=423); main text; image-checked.

> Wo die Wortbildung an keine so feste Form gebunden ist,

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

**Relations:** [Verbindung](terms-s-z.md#verbindung) (contrast: Vocabulary formation versus combining discourse.).

<a id="wortclasse"></a>
## Wortclasse

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** word classification. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Wortclassen.

### 1. Wortclasse

**English:** word class.

A class of words treated in accordance with the general organization of expression.

**Rule:** Keep the class distinct from its marker and from the semantic content of an individual word.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:456 / PDF 466 / scan leaf 470](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=466); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> die einzelnen Wortclassen behandelt

<a id="wortelement"></a>
## Wortelement

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** lexicon. **Role:** contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Wortelemente.

### 1. local sense

**English:** word element.

Elements of words whose stock does not exhaust the broad mental process of language including its effect on thought and feeling.

**Rule:** Use word element for the defined local use.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:374 / PDF 384 / scan leaf 388](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=384); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> seiner weitesten Ausdehnung genommen, nicht bloss in der Beziehung derselben auf die Rede und den Vorrath ihrer Wortelemente, sondern auch in der Beziehung auf ihren Einfluss auf das Denk- und Empfindungsvermögen. Der ganze Gang kommt in Betrachtung, auf dem sie, von dem Geiste ausgehend, auf den Geist zurückwirkt,

<a id="wortform"></a>
## Wortform

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** grammar, linguistic analysis. **Role:** grammatical label, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Wortformen.

### 1. local sense

**English:** word form.

Word forms needed for connecting thoughts in discourse, examined by general grammar as only part of language.

**Rule:** Use word form for the defined local use.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:373 / PDF 383 / scan leaf 387](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=383); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> mit der logischen Zergliederung der Rede und der Untersuchung der zur Verknüpfung der Gedanken in dieser nöthigen Wortformen ; also zunächst mit einem Erzeugniss der Sprache und daher nur mit einem Theile von dieser. Man hat zwar hie und da dem Begriff der allgemeinen Grammatik eine grössere Ausdehnung

### 2. Wortform

**English:** word form.

A word form collected in grammatical investigation; inventorying forms is insufficient without a guiding organization.

**Rule:** Do not equate a list of word forms with the complete organization of a language.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:449 / PDF 459 / scan leaf 463](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=459); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> auch aus der mühsamsten Aufsuchung aller Wortformen zusammenzutragen

**Relations:** [grammatische Form](terms-f-k.md#grammatische-form) (related: An inventory of word forms does not by itself supply the full grammatical organization.).

**English:** word wholes / whole words.

Whole words regarded as units requiring their own audible unity.

**Rule:** Preserve the unity of each word rather than a loose collection of words.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:405 / PDF 415 / scan leaf 419](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=415); main text; image-checked.

> die Wortganze durch besondre Toneinheit zu charakterisiren,

<a id="wortgruppe"></a>
## Wortgruppe

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** discourse organization, linguistic analysis. **Role:** contextual vocabulary, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Wortgruppe, Wortgruppen.

### 1. local sense

**English:** word group.

A word group whose written sense can be understood before one is certain of its alphabetic composition, analogized with taking a word as an indivisible whole.

**Rule:** Use word group for the defined local use.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:384 / PDF 394 / scan leaf 398](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=394); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> allerdings auch als untheilbares Ganzes genommen werden, wie man auch in der Schrift wohl den Sinn einer Wortgruppe erkennt, ohne noch ihrer alphabetischen Zusammensetzung gewiss zu seyn, und es wäre möglich, dass die Seele des Kindes in den ersten Anfängen des Verstehens so verführe. So wie aber nicht

### 2. Wortgruppe

**English:** word group.

An internally closed grouping of words within a period, with restricted rearrangement.

**Rule:** Use word group for the attested Wortgruppe here.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:447 / PDF 457 / scan leaf 461](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=457); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Die Periode besteht in derselben aus einzelnen, in sich geschlossenen Wortgruppen

<a id="wortstellung"></a>
## Wortstellung

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** grammatical realization. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Wortstellung.

### 1. Wortstellung

**English:** word order.

Word order considered as a possible grammatical indication only under a stringent fixedness and independent-anchor condition.

**Rule:** Retain all conditions, completed on printed 470: a fixed point must be recognizable independently of order.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:469 / PDF 479 / scan leaf 483](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=479); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> die durch Wortstellung aus, wenn sie absolut fest, unverrückbar und sich auf einen festen, nicht wieder nur an seiner Stellung

**Reading note:** Image corrects the supplied OCR; both readings retained.

**Relations:** [grammatische Form](terms-f-k.md#grammatische-form) (realization_condition: Only the stringent fixed-order test can qualify as pure formal indication.).

**Comparative control:** [printed 319; PDF 329](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf). Fixed order is said not to be sufficient by itself: lexical meaning and discourse context also support understanding. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

<a id="wortverbindung"></a>
## Wortverbindung

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** linguistic analysis. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Wortverbindung.

### 1. Wortverbindung

**English:** word connection / connection of words.

The intended determinate manner of connecting words, which can have its own non-substantive sign.

**Rule:** Keep the intended connection apart from its sign and from newly introduced substantive content.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:464 / PDF 474 / scan leaf 478](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=474); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Wo die in der Rede beabsichtete bestimmte Art der Wortverbindung ihr eignes, keinen neuen Sachbegriff herbeiführendes Zeichen findet

**Reading note:** Image corrects the supplied OCR; both readings retained.

**Relations:** [Redeverbindung](terms-l-r.md#redeverbindung) (related: A determinate connecting operation is considered within whole speech.).

<a id="wurzelwort"></a>
## Wurzelwort

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** word formation. **Role:** source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Wurzelwörtern.

### 1. Root word with both grammatical distribution and lexical derivational roles; not necessarily the universal origin of all other words

**English:** root word.

Root word with both grammatical distribution and lexical derivational roles; not necessarily the universal origin of all other words.

**Rule:** Do not force every lexical item under assumed roots.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:440 / PDF 450 / scan leaf 454](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=450); main text; image-checked.

### 1. local sense

### 2. written reminder-sign

**English:** sign.

Written reminder of a speech sound.

**Rule:** Differentiate this ordinary reminder-sign from the later argument about words as signs.

> ein an diesen Schall erinnerndes Zeichen erhält,

Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

### 3. sign with independently existing designated content

**English:** sign.

A sign standing for what can exist independently of it; contrasted with a word whose concept receives completion through it.

**Rule:** A word may serve a sign’s purpose without being reducible to this relation.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:428 / PDF 438 / scan leaf 442](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=438); main text; image-checked.

> dass das Bezeichnete ein von seinem Zeichen unabhängiges Daseyn hat,

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

### 4. empty sign contrasted with designated content

**English:** sign.

A sign contrasted with substituting what is designated for it; the immediately parallel phrase contrasts putting a concept in place of form.

**Rule:** Preserve both parallels in§141. The formal reading is supported by this local opposition, not by importing§147’s strict criterion into the fragment.

**Caution:** Empty is the source’s description in this polemic, not a claim that speakers cannot understand or that all signs lack contribution.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:461 / PDF 471 / scan leaf 475](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=471); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> so oft den Begriff an die Stelle der Form, Bezeichnetes an die Stelle des leeren Zeichens setzen

### 5. lexical sign under dictionary abstraction

**English:** sign.

A word considered as a sign for concepts and objects from the dictionary standpoint used in the comparison.

**Rule:** Retain the analytic dictionary standpoint; this is not a blanket equation of every living word with a detachable label.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:464 / PDF 474 / scan leaf 478](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=474); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> so wie die Wörter, wie sie im Wörterbuch stehen, Zeichen der Begriffe und Gegenstände sind

**Reading note:** Image corrects the supplied OCR; both readings retained.

**Comparative control:** [Adelung1811, Das Zeichen; §2](https://lexika.digitale-sammlungen.de/adelung/lemma/bsb00009134_9_0_278). A sensible item intended to evoke a representation, with mark, indication and sign variants. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.

<a id="zusammenfliessen"></a>
## zusammenfliessen

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text; editorial infinitive headword. **Domains:** historical contact and geographical grouping. **Role:** contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** zusammengeflossen.

### 1. geographical convergence of different languages

**English:** converge / flow together.

Different languages have come together within the same continent; the statement does not itself require the languages to have blended into one.

**Rule:** Use converge in§1; keep the geographical grouping distinct from an asserted mixing of the languages.

**Caution:** The printed parallel uses a different mixing expression. Do not substitute it into this sentence or infer a revision chronology. The infinitive is an editorial lookup lemma; the attested form is zusammengeflossen.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:364 / PDF 374 / scan leaf 376](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=374); main text; coordinator image-checked short quotation.

> In Europa, Asien und Afrika sind so verschiedene Sprachen zusammengeflossen

**Reading note:** Line-end division rejoined; preserves the source’s past participle. The broader packet and subsequent§7 distinguish geographical and historical inquiry.

### 2. a language formed through confluence of others

**English:** flow together / coalesce / arise through confluence.

In the discussion of lexical richness, a language such as English is described as having flowed together from different languages, leaving expressions with different origins.

**Rule:** Here the language itself is the result of confluence; do not carry over§1’s merely geographical grouping as a universal sense.

**Caution:** Humboldt’s analysis and historical example are retained as source claims. The difference lies in the local relation, not in an invariant ban on translating this verb with a mixing-related expression.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:436 / PDF 446 / scan leaf 450](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=446); main text; coordinator image-checked short quotation.

> Sogar wenn Sprachen, wie die Englische, aus verschiedenen zusammengeflossen, für gewisse Begriffe zwei bloss durch ihren Ursprung verschiedene Ausdrücke

**Reading note:** Quote bounded before the English examples; no OCR-corrupt liberty form is certified by this quotation.

**Relations:** [Vermischung](terms-s-z.md#vermischung) (distinguish_from: Convergence in this sentence need not assert the mixing separately discussed in historical passages.).

<a id="zusammenfuegung"></a>

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Zusammenfügung.

**English:** combination.

Combination of basic parts into wholes which tend to become parts of further wholes, common to mental form and articulation.

> die Articulation, wie in einem verknüpfenden Mittel begegnen, ist, dass beide ihr Gebiet in Grundtheile zerlegen, deren Zusammenfügung lauter solche Ganze bildet, welche das Streben in sich tragen, Theile neuer Ganze zu werden. Ausser jener Gewalt ist aber auch in dem Geiste ein, sich den Sprachwerkzeugen selbst mittheilender

> in der Zusammenfügung der Rede gleichförmig erscheinen.

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. ### 3. Zusammenfügung

**English:** joining together / composition.

Composition or joining of speech from words, contrasted methodologically with analyzing a prior whole.

**Rule:** Use joining together for the attested Zusammenfügung here.

<a id="zusammenhang"></a>
## Zusammenhang

**Scope:** Grundzüge main text. **Domains:** historical relationship, internal organization, linguistic analysis. **Role:** contextual vocabulary, source terminology or contextual vocabulary.

**Checked/rejoined source forms:** Zusammenhang, Zusammenhange.

### 1. local sense

**English:** connection.

Historical connection among languages distinguished from likeness due merely to shared human capacities.

**Rule:** Keep historical connectedness distinct from a presumed single original language.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:393 / PDF 403 / scan leaf 407](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=403); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> sollte es auch wenig fruchtbar für die Anwendung seyn, der Grundsatz ausgesprochen ist, nach welchem der geschichtliche Zusammenhang der Sprachen von derjenigen Gleichartigkeit unterschieden werden kann, welche bloss in der Gleichartigkeit der menschlichen Anlagen zu suchen ist. Denn gesetzt dass die Amerikanischen Nationen einen heimathlichen Ursprung gehabt, und

**Reading note:** Image correction: 'w^enig' → 'wenig'.

### 2. internal interdependence

**English:** interconnection.

The tight interconnection of parts within language and of language with nation, through which distinctive world-view arises.

**Rule:** Identify internal/reciprocal relations rather than automatically historical contact.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:387 / PDF 397 / scan leaf 401](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=397); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Sprache eine eigenthümliche Weltansicht. Dieser Ausdruck überschreitet auf keine Weise das Mass der einfachen Wahrheit. Denn der Zusammenhang aller Theile der Sprache unter einander, und der ganzen Sprache mit der Nation ist so enge, dass, wenn einmal diese Wechselwirkung eine bestimmte Richtung angiebt, daraus nothwendig durchgängige Eigenthümlichkeit hervorgehen

### 3. internal relation of a linguistic system

**English:** connection / interconnection.

Internal connection of words through formation and sound patterns, to be established before interlanguage comparison.

**Rule:** Not simply surrounding text.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:415 / PDF 425 / scan leaf 429](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=425); main text; image-checked.

> Aus diesem inneren Zusammenhange der Wörter, auch in ihren Lauten,

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

### 4. context determining interpretation

**English:** context / conceptual context.

Contextual connection of concepts that forces a more specific reading than a word’s simple lexical sense.

**Rule:** Do not turn every context-induced reading into a new dictionary sense.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:425 / PDF 435 / scan leaf 439](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=435); main text; image-checked.

> der Zusammenhang der Begriffe den natürlichen und einfachen Sinn des Worts hinüberzwängt.

**Reading note:** Image confirms print wording; rejoined print line divisions and/or corrected OCR: OCR preserves the contiguous locator window; checked_quote is the bounded supporting clause inspected on the image. Rejoined printed line divisions and excluded any neighboring margin/apparatus text.

### 5. contextual connection

**English:** context / connection.

Contextual connection contributing to comprehension alongside word order and particles.

**Rule:** Keep contextual recovery distinct from specifically grammatical marking.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:461 / PDF 471 / scan leaf 475](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=471); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> Stellung, Partikeln und der Zusammenhang eröffnen das Verständniss

### 6. systemic interconnection

**English:** interconnection / coherence.

The interconnection or coherence of a language’s organized whole, distinct from immediate semantic context.

**Rule:** Do not force context as the English equivalent when the entire system is the bearer.

**Evidence (direct occurrence):** [GS V:450 / PDF 460 / scan leaf 464](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf#page=460); main text; image-checked short quotation.

> in den Zusammenhang des Organismus einer einzelnen Sprache

**Comparative control:** [printed 319; PDF 329](../../../sources/raw/gs-v-1906.pdf). Fixed order is said not to be sufficient by itself: lexical meaning and discourse context also support understanding. A comparative control for this named term; the individual local sense is supported by its own primary attestation.
